(譯：完全不提供任何無負擔擔保，僅「默示」轉讓人擁有轉讓產權權力的契據是什麼？)
A. bargain and sale deed (買賣契據) [正確答案]
B. land patent (土地專利證書/政府原始授權書)
C. quitclaim deed (放棄權利契據)
D. warranty deed (擔保契據)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (bargain and sale deed)
概念拆解： Bargain and Sale Deed 的核心特徵是：它默示（Implies） 轉讓人對該房產擁有所有權或控制權，但完全不提供任何無負擔的法律擔保（No warranties against encumbrances）。若日後發現房屋帶有未知的債務或留置權，買方無法憑此契據向賣方索賠。
選項錯誤原因：
C (quitclaim deed)： 連「轉讓人擁有所有權」的默示都不提供（只轉讓當下可能有的權益）。
D (warranty deed)： 提供完整且強大的無瑕疵與無負擔擔保。
6.Which instrument of conveyance provides the grantee with the greatest protection?
(譯：哪一種轉讓工具能給予受讓者（買方）最大/最高等級的保護？)
A. warranty deed (（一般）擔保契據) [正確答案]
B. bargain and sale deed (買賣契據)
C. grant deed (轉讓契據)
D. quitclaim deed (放棄權利契據)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (warranty deed / General Warranty Deed)
概念拆解： General Warranty Deed 是法律上保護力最強的契據。它包含了完整的六大法定誓約（Six Covenants），承諾產權毫無瑕疵，且擔保範圍追溯至該物業歷史上所有的前手業主（All previous owners）。
保護力強弱排序（考試必背）：
General Warranty Deed (最高) ➔ Special Warranty Deed / Grant Deed ➔ Bargain & Sale Deed ➔ Quitclaim Deed (最低)。
7.The covenant that assures that the grantor holds the title being conveyed in the deed is the:
(譯：契據中向買方保證「轉讓人確實合法擁有該轉讓產權」的誓約是：)
A. covenant of seisin (所有權誓約 / 占有誓約) [正確答案]
B. covenant against encumbrances (無負擔誓約)
C. covenant of the quiet enjoyment (平靜享有誓約)
D. covenant of further assurances (進一步保證誓約)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (covenant of seisin)
概念拆解： Covenant of Seisin 是 General Warranty Deed 中的第一大誓約，轉讓人聲明並保證自己合法占有（Seised） 該產權，並擁有合法的權力將其出售轉讓。
其他選項誓約對比：
B (against encumbrances)： 保證除契據中載明的項目外，房屋沒有其他未揭露的留置權或負擔。
C (quiet enjoyment)： 保證買方購入後不會被擁有更高產權優先權的第三方驅逐或干擾。
D (further assurances)： 保證轉讓人未來願意簽署任何必要的法律文件來補正產權瑕疵。
8.What is a legal process by which a court determines the validity of a will and establishes the assets of a decedent and who will inherit those assets?
(譯：由法院確認遺囑效力、釐清逝者資產並確定誰將繼承該資產的法定程序稱為什麼？)
A. probate (遺囑認證 / 遺產法庭程序) [正確答案]
B. testate (有遺囑的狀態)
C. intestate (無遺囑的狀態)
D. adverse possession (時效取得 / 逆向占有)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (probate)
概念拆解： Probate（遺產法庭審理/遺囑認證程序） 是專門的司法程序。法院在程序中審查遺囑的合法性、指定清算執行人、結清逝者債務，並監督不動產與財產合法過戶給法定繼承人。
選項錯誤原因：
B (testate)： 形容人去世時「留有合法遺囑」的法律狀態（名詞/形容詞）。
C (intestate)： 形容人去世時「未留遺囑」的法律狀態。
D (adverse possession)： 指未經許可長期公開佔用他人土地而依法取得所有權的法律機制。
9.A deed specifies that the property being transferred comes with an easement (and describes the easement). What is an example of?
(譯：契據中特別說明並描述轉讓的物業附帶一項地役權，這属于下列哪一個範例？)
A. reservation or exception (保留或例外條款) [正確答案]
B. granting clause (轉讓條款)
C. consideration (對價)
D. habendum clause (產權處分/權能條款)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (reservation or exception)
概念拆解： Reservations and Exceptions（保留與例外條款） 用於契據中排除某些產權權利，或載明物業上已存在、會影響買方完全使用權的限制（例如：地役權 Easements、礦業權保留或土地使用限制）。
選項錯誤原因：
B (granting clause)： 契據中表達轉讓意圖的核心句子（如 "I hereby grant and convey..."）。
C (consideration)： 契約成立的金錢或價值對價。
D (habendum clause)： 標誌詞為 "To have and to hold"，定義買方取得的權利種類（如 Fee Simple）。
10.What is delivery and acceptance?
(譯：什麼是「交付與接受（Delivery and Acceptance）」？)
A. required actions for the proper execution of a deed, involving delivery of the deed by the grantor and acceptance by the grantee (契據正式生效的法定必要動作，包含轉讓人交付契據與受讓人接受物業) [正確答案]
B. an instrument that conveys public land to an individual (將公有土地轉讓給個人的法律工具 — 應為 Land Patent)
C. a deed that contains no covenants or warranties, although ownership is implied (不含任何誓約或擔保但默示所有權的契據 — 應為 Bargain and Sale Deed)
D. the person who transfers title to real property in a real estate transaction (在交易中轉讓不動產產權的人 — 應為 Grantor)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A
概念拆解： 契據單純簽字（Signed）還不夠！必須經過轉讓人意圖將產權移轉的「Delivery（交付）」以及受讓人的「Acceptance（接受）」，產權移轉（Title Transfer）才在法律上正式完成與生效。
11.What is a description of a property that is distinct and precise enough to distinguish it from all other properties?
(譯：對物業進行極為精準且獨一無二的描述，足以將其與世界上所有其他物業區分開來，稱為什麼？)
A. legal description (不動產法定描述) [正確答案]
B. bargain and sale deed (買賣契據)
C. public grant (公有土地撥給/授權)
D. block and lot (街區與地號法 — 僅為 Legal Description 的其中一種形式)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (legal description)
概念拆解： Legal Description（法定描述） 是法律文件上唯一的官方標準界定方式（如 Metes & Bounds、Lot & Block、Government Survey）。一般的街道地址（Street Address）可能重複或隨政府規劃更換，唯有 Legal Description 能精準且唯一地標示地表上的特定界線。
12.An agent's client is concerned about the marketability of their title. The agent advises that if they want their title to be marketable, they need to focus on clearing up all:
(譯：經紀人的客戶擔心其產權的可銷售性。經紀人建議若要確保產權為可銷售產權，必須重點清理所有的：)
A. significant encumbrances and liens (重大負擔與留置權) [正確答案]
B. mortgages (抵押權)
C. chains of title (產權連鎖)
D. title plant limitations (產權資料庫限制)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (significant encumbrances and liens)
概念拆解： Marketable Title（可銷售產權） 的法定定義是：產權清晰、沒有嚴重瑕疵，且不存在未解決的重大負擔（Encumbrances）與留置權（Liens，如欠稅、法院扣押等），讓理性買家可以在未來安心持有或再次出售。
選項錯誤原因：
B (mortgages)： Mortgages 屬於 Liens 的一種，但選項 A 包含了更廣泛的所有重大 Lien 與 Encumbrance，故 A 為最佳且最完整的答案。
13.What does recording a deed create in the public record?
(譯：將契據登錄於公共紀錄中會產生什麼？)
A. constructive notice (擬制告知 / 推定告知) [正確答案]
B. actual notice (實際告知)
C. inquiry notice (調查告知)
D. abstract of title (產權摘要)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (constructive notice)
概念拆解： 將契據送至 County Recorder 辦理登錄（Recording），即在法律上建立了 Constructive Notice（擬制告知）。法律直接假設社會大眾（包括潛在買家）均有管道查詢並知悉該產權的異動情況。
14.You are an attorney who works for a title company. You perform a title search for a client, analyzing the chain of title, clouds on the title, and looking for any title issues. You label this search a(n):
(譯：你是產權公司的律師，為客戶進行產權檢索，分析產權連鎖、產權瑕疵並尋找潛在問題。你會將這份檢索成果標註為：)
A. abstract of title (產權摘要) [正確答案]
B. title plant (產權資料庫)
C. equitable title (衡平法產權 / 權益產權)
D. marketable title (可銷售產權)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (abstract of title)
概念拆解： 將產權檢索（Title Search）所查到的歷史演變（Chain of title）、未決留置權與瑕疵（Clouds on title）等所有檔案資料，按時間順序整理成一份綜合歷史報告，這份報告即稱為 Abstract of Title（產權摘要）。
選項錯誤原因：
B (title plant)： 產權公司內部儲存歷史紀錄的資料庫機房/設施。
C (equitable title)： 買賣合約簽署後、正式過戶拿 Deed 前，買方獲得的法律權益。
15.A grandfather conveys his property to his granddaughter. The deed contains: (1) the granddaughter's name; (2) a statement that he has received $10 and the granddaughter's love and affection; and (3) a statement that the property is conveyed to the granddaughter “to have and to hold.” Identify, in order, these elements of the deed?
(譯：祖父將房產轉讓給孫女。契據包含：(1) 孫女的名字；(2) 收到 $10 元以及孫女的愛與關懷之聲明；(3) 房產轉讓給孫女「以擁有並持有」之聲明。請依序識別這三個契據要素？)
A. grantee; consideration; habendum clause (受讓人；對價；產權處分/權能條款) [正確答案]
B. grantee; consideration; grantor (受讓人；對價；轉讓人)
C. grantor; habendum clause; legal description (轉讓人；權能條款；法定描述)
D. grantee; acknowledgment; habendum clause (受讓人；公證認可；權能條款)
【詳細解析】
正確答案：A (grantee; consideration; habendum clause)
元素 (1) 孫女的名字 ➔ Grantee（受讓人）： 接受房產轉讓的人。
元素 (2) $10 + Love and affection ➔ Consideration（對價）： 契約成立的法定對價（親情贈與常用 Good Consideration，即愛與關懷）。
元素 (3) "To have and to hold" ➔ Habendum Clause（權能條款）： 契據中明確定義受讓人獲得何種權利範圍（如 Fee Simple 或 Life Estate）的專屬標誌性條款。




16.What is the difference between actual and constructive notice?
(譯：實際告知（Actual Notice）與擬制告知/推定告知（Constructive Notice）有何不同？)
A. Constructive notice is direct or first-hand knowledge... (敘述顛倒，誤將 Constructive notice 說成第一手知識)
B. Constructive notice is the assumption that a diligent individual can search the public record to gain knowledge; actual notice is direct or first-hand knowledge. (擬制告知是假設勤勉的個人可透過檢索公共紀錄獲取知識；實際告知則是直接或第一手的親身知悉) [正確答案]
C. Constructive notice is part of giving notice and actual notice isn't. (錯誤：兩者皆為告知/通知的形式)
D. Actual notice does not involve searching public records and constructive notice does. (描述過於片面且不精準，非最完整定義)
🔍 核心考點解析：Actual Notice vs. Constructive Notice
不動產產權（Title）與登錄（Recording）章節中，區分這兩種「告知/知悉」方式是極高頻的考題：
Constructive Notice（擬制告知 / 推定告知 / 法律推定知悉）：
定義： 法律上假設/推定大眾「應該要知曉」的事實。
實現方式： 將房屋產權契據（Deed）、抵押權（Mortgage）或留置權（Lien）依法登錄於縣政府公共紀錄（Public Records），或是買家對房屋進行實際佔有/入住（Physical Possession）。
法理： 只要文件已公開登錄，法律即視為全世界的人（包含潛在買家）都已收到通知。若買家沒去查紀錄而受損，不能以「我不知道」為由抗辯。
Actual Notice（實際告知 / 親身知悉）：
定義： 當事人實際、直接親眼看見、親耳聽到或親身接觸到的第一手資訊與事實（Direct or First-hand knowledge）。
例子： 買家親自讀了產權報告（Title Report）、賣方當面告訴買家「這房子有抵押權」、或是買家親眼看到屋後有一條未登錄的通行巷道（Easement）。
💡 考試直覺反射金句：
Constructive Notice ＝ 登錄公共紀錄（Public Records）或實際佔有，法律推定你知情！
Actual Notice ＝ 親眼看見、親耳聽聞（First-hand Knowledge），你真的知道！




17.When applicable, what is the best form of title evidence?
(譯：在適用/採行的情況下，哪一種是最佳的產權證明（Evidence of Title）形式？)
A. Torrens certificate (托倫斯產權登記證書) [正確答案]
B. abstract of title (產權摘要)
C. title insurance (產權保險)
D. attorney's opinion of title (律師產權意見書)
🔍 核心考點解析：四種 Evidence of Title 的排位與差異
不動產交易中需要提供「產權證明（Evidence of Title）」來證明賣方擁有可銷售的合法產權（Marketable Title）。在考試體系中，這幾種證明的優先級與權限如下：
Torrens Certificate（第一名 / 最高等級）：
在採用托倫斯登記制（Torrens System）的州/地區，土地產權由政府機關直接審查、登記並頒發證書。
優勢： 政府登記冊上的紀錄具有不可爭議的最高法律效力（Unassailable evidence），只要適用，它就是法律上「絕對最佳（Absolute Best）」的產權證明。
Title Insurance（第二名 / 最常見）：
如果該地區未使用 Torrens System，產權保險（Title Insurance） 是實際市場交易中最常用且次佳（Second Best）的證明方式，因為它提供實質的財務理賠保障。
Abstract of Title & Attorney's Opinion of Title（摘要與律師意見書）：
Abstract of Title（產權摘要）： 僅為歷代產權移轉與紀錄的歷史摘要清單，本身不保證產權沒有瑕疵。
Attorney's Opinion of Title（律師產權意見書）： 由律師閱讀摘要後出具的法律意見，雖具參考價值，但若未來出現未發現的隱藏瑕疵，律師通常不承擔保險理賠責任。
💡 考試直覺反射金句：
產權證明（Evidence of Title）排行榜：Torrens Certificate 第一（Best） ➔ Title Insurance 第二！
18.There are some things a title policy will NOT cover. For example:
(譯：產權保險單（Title Insurance Policy）有些事項是「不予承保（NOT cover / Exclusions）」的。例如：)
A. losses from ownership problems from before the property was purchased
(在購買物業之前就已存在的產權所有權問題所造成的損失)
B. absence of right of access to and from the land
(缺乏進出該土地的合法通行權)
C. liens for labor and material from before the policy effective date that the borrower didn’t agree to pay
(在保單生效日之前發生、且借款人並未同意支付的勞務與材料留置權/技工留置權)
D. problems due to a borrower's failure to pay their mortgage
(因借款人自身未能按時支付房貸而引發的問題) [正確答案]
🔍 深度解析：產權保險（Title Insurance）承保與除外責任
產權保險（Title Insurance）的核心宗旨是：保障買方或貸款機構「在保單生效日以前（Past Events）」就已存在但未被發現的產權瑕疵。
D（正確答案 — 不予承保）：
產權保險保障的是「產權是否乾淨合法（Clear Title）」，而不是個人財務保險。如果買家在買房後自己不繳房貸（Failure to pay mortgage）導致房屋被銀行法拍（Foreclosure），這屬於買家在保單生效之後的個人財務違約行為，產權保險絕對不予承保（NOT cover）。
為什麼其他選項都是「會承保（Covered）」的項目？
A 錯誤（會承保）： 承保「購屋前（Before purchased）就存在的過往所有權問題」（如：前一手偽造簽名、未出現的隱藏繼承人爭產等），這正是產權保險最主要、最核心的承保項目！
B 錯誤（會承保）： 標準產權保險單（Standard Title Policy）包含對「合法通行權（Right of Access）」的保障。如果買下土地後發現法律上完全沒有任何道路或地役權可以進出該土地（無路可走的袋地），產權保險會提供理賠或法律賠償。
C 錯誤（會承保）： 在保單生效日之前就已產生、但未登錄或未被發現的技工留置權（Mechanic's Liens，如前屋主欠水電工或裝修商的材料費），只要買家事前不知情且未同意承擔，產權保險通常會予以保障 cover。
💡 考試直覺反射金句：
產權保險保「過去的產權瑕疵（Past title defects）」，不保「買家未來的欠債違約（Borrower's failure to pay）」！
19.Which of these is an example of the doctrine of laches?
(譯：下列哪一個選項是「懈怠原則（Doctrine of Laches）」的範例？)
A. Oswald bought two adjoining parcels of property and built a home twice the size normally allowed in the middle of them.
(Oswald 購買了兩塊相鄰的土地，並在其正中間建造了一棟兩倍於正常允許尺寸的房屋。)
B. A neighborhood association created rules designed to keep people of a certain religion from buying homes in the neighborhood.
(社區協會制定了旨在防止特定宗教信仰者在社區內買房的規定。)
C. Henry's tree grew into his neighbor's fence, and now his neighbor wants to sell him that tiny slice of property.
(Henry 的樹長到了鄰居的籬笆內，現在鄰居想把那小塊土地賣給他。)
D. An HOA allowed a neighbor to keep prohibited chickens for years, so now the prohibition against chickens is not enforceable.
(社區管委會（HOA）多年來一直允許某位鄰居飼養被禁止的雞隻，因此現在該禁雞規定已無法強制執行。) [正確答案]
【詳細解析】
🔍 核心概念：懈怠原則（Doctrine of Laches）
Doctrine of Laches（懈怠原則 / 權利睡眠原則） 是衡平法（Equitable Law）中的一項重要法律防禦機制：
基本定義：
如果擁有主張權利的一方（例如 HOA 或鄰居），在知情的情況下無故拖延、長期怠於行使其法律權利（Unreasonable Delay），導致違規方產生了合理依賴，那麼該權利人未來將喪失透過法院強制執行該權利的資格。俗稱「法律不保護在權利上睡覺的人」。
選項 D 為何正確：
社區管委會（HOA）明知條款禁止養雞（Deed Restriction / CC&R Violation），卻「多年來」置之不理（怠於行使權利）。當他們多年後突然想要開罰或強制提告驅逐時，被告可以主張 Doctrine of Laches 作為抗辯，法院通常會認定 HOA 已經因怠於執行而喪失了該條款的強制執行力。
其他選項錯誤解析：
A 錯誤： 這屬於建築分區法規（Zoning Law）或違規建築問題，非 Laches。
B 錯誤： 這是違反《聯邦公平住房法》（Fair Housing Act）的非法歧視條款（Illegal Discrimination）。
C 錯誤： 這屬於越界侵占（Encroachment）或邊界糾紛（Boundary Dispute）。
💡 考試直覺反射金句：
Doctrine of Laches ＝ 長期拖延不提告（Unreasonable Delay）➔ 喪失條款強制執行力（Lose the right to enforce）


20.A seller sells their home to a buyer. At a basic level, what roles are the buyer and the seller playing in a real estate transaction?賣方將房屋出售給買方。在基礎層面上，買方與賣方在不動產交易中分別扮演什麼法律角色？
A. The buyer is the testator, the seller is the legatee.(買方是立遺囑人，賣方是受遺贈人)
B. The seller is the legatee, the buyer is the testator.(賣方是受遺贈人，買方是立遺囑人)
C. The seller is the grantor, the buyer is the grantee.(賣方是轉讓人/授權人，買方是受讓人/受權人) [正確答案]
D. The buyer is the grantor, the seller is the grantee.(買方是轉讓人/授權人，賣方是受讓人/受權人 — 角色顛倒)
【詳細解析】
🔍 核心概念：不動產交易中的角色術語（-OR vs. -EE）
不動產法律英語中有一個非常重要的「字尾記憶法則」：
字尾 -OR ＝ 給予/讓出權利的人（The Giver）
字尾 -EE ＝ 接收/獲得權利的人（The Receiver）
在房屋買賣過戶時，轉讓契據（Deed）是由賣方簽署並將房屋產權（Title）移轉給買方：
Grantor（轉讓人 / 授權人 / 賣方）：
出售房產、簽署契據並將產權轉讓出去（Grants/Conveys the property）的一方 ➔ Seller（賣方）。
Grantee（受讓人 / 受權人 / 買方）：
支付對價並接收產權（Receives the property）的一方 ➔ Buyer（買方）。
❌ 其他選項的干擾術語（遺囑與遺產法律用語）：
Testator（立遺囑人）： 撰寫並立下遺囑的人。
Legatee（受遺贈人）： 透過遺囑獲得動產/個人財產（Personal Property / Legacy）的人。

💡 考試直覺反射金句：
賣方轉讓給買方 ＝ Seller is the Grantor (-OR)，Buyer is the Grantee (-EE)！


21.A buyer purchases a property and then records the deed in the public record. What is this an example of?買方購買了一處物業，隨後將契據登錄於公共紀錄中。這是下列哪一項的範例？
A. actual notice(實際告知 / 親自知悉)
B. constructive notice(擬制告知 / 推定告知 / 公開登錄告知) [正確答案]
C. abstract of title(產權摘要)
D. inquiry notice(查詢告知 / 調查告知)
【詳細解析】
核心概念：Constructive Notice（擬制告知 / 推定告知）
在不動產法規與產權過戶（Title & Recording）章節中，「告知通知（Notice）」 的法律效力是極高頻的考點：
Constructive Notice（擬制告知 / 推定告知）：
定義： 當買方或產權人將與房產相關的法律文件（如 Deed、Mortgage、Lien）依法送交縣政府登記處（County Recorder）完成公開登錄（Recording in the public record）時，法律即自動推定（Legal Fiction / Assumption）全世界每個人都「應該知道」這項產權異動。
核心邏輯： 只要文件進了公家檔案庫，法律就認為你「有管道去查，沒去查是你家的事」，直接賦予全社會法律上的通知效力。

其他選項的辨析：
A (Actual Notice)： 指當事人實際、親眼看見或親耳聽到的第一手確切消息（例如：賣方親口告訴買方房屋有抵押權，或親眼看到有人住在那裡）。
C (Abstract of Title)： 是一份按時間順序整理物業歷史產權變動與留置權的彙整報告（History Summary Report），本身不是告知形式。
D (Inquiry Notice)： 指基於現狀（例如看到房子有在施工的痕跡或有人居住），一個理性的正常人會產生懷疑並進一步主動調查（Inquiry）所獲得的法律推論。
考試直覺反射金句：
只要動作是「Recording in public records（登錄公共紀錄）」➔ 答案必定是 Constructive Notice（擬制告知）！


22.Sally and Jorge are a married couple who own a house together. The couple has one adult daughter, Emilia. When Jorge dies intestate, who receives the house?Sally 與 Jorge 是一對夫妻，兩人共同擁有的一棟房屋。這對夫妻有一個成年女兒 Emilia。當 Jorge 未留遺囑過世（dies intestate）時，誰會取得這棟房屋？
A. Sally receives one-third; Emilia receives the rest.
(Sally 取得三分之一，Emilia 取得其餘部分)
B. Both Sally and Emilia receive one-half.
(Sally 與 Emilia 各取得二分之一)
C. Sally receives the house.
(Sally 取得整棟房屋) [正確答案]
D. Emilia receives the house.
(Emilia 取得整棟房屋)
【詳細解析】
🔍 核心概念：夫妻共同所有財產與無遺囑繼承（Married Couple & Community Property / Joint Tenancy）
這是一道在美國不動產測驗（特別是 California 或採用 Community Property 夫妻共同財產制 的州）中極為常見的法律原則考題：
預設所有權型態（Title Ownership）：
在不動產測驗中，當題目敘述為「夫妻共同擁有房屋（a married couple who own a house together）」時，法律上預設的產權型態通常包含 Right of Survivorship（生存者取得權）（如 Joint Tenancy，或在加州等地區為帶生存權的 Community Property）。
只要帶有生存者取得權，當其中一位配偶過世時，死者的產權會自動歸屬於存活的配偶（Surviving Spouse），完全不進入法院遺囑認證（Probate），也不受無遺囑繼承法分配給子女。
無遺囑繼承法（Intestate Succession）下的夫妻共同財產規則：
退一步而言，即使依據採用夫妻共同財產制（Community Property）州的無遺囑繼承法，當已婚者無遺囑過世（Intestate），其夫妻共同財產（Community Property）中的 100% 份額將全數自動歸屬於存活的配偶（Sally）。
只有當遺產屬於逝者的個人獨立財產（Separate Property）時，才會在配偶與子女（Emilia）之間進行拆分（如拆分為配偶與子女各 1/2，或配偶 1/3 子女 2/3）。但本題明確說明是夫妻共同擁有（own together）的房屋，屬於共同財產，因此 Sally 取得 100% 的房屋所有權。
考試直覺反射金句：夫妻共同擁有的房屋（Owned together），配偶過世無遺囑 ➔ 存活配偶（Sally）取得整棟房屋！


23.A borrower defaulted on their loan to National ABC Bank. In response, National ABC Bank foreclosed on their house. What is this an example of?一位借款人違約未償還 National ABC 銀行貸款。作為回應，National ABC 銀行對該房屋實施了法拍（Foreclosure）。這是下列哪一項的範例？
A. involuntary alienation(非自願讓與 / 非自願轉讓) [正確答案]
B. voluntary alienation(自願讓與 / 自願轉讓)
C. eminent domain(徵收權 / 土地徵收)
D. escheat(充公權 / 無主財產歸公)
【詳細解析】
🔍 核心概念：Alienation（產權讓與/轉讓的分類）
在不動產法規中，Alienation 指的是「產權/所有權從一人移轉給另一人」的法律過程，主要分為兩大類：
Involuntary Alienation（非自願讓與）：
定義： 指業主在非出自本意/未經其自由意思表示（Without owner's consent）的情況下，因法律程序、債務追討、自然災害或政府權力而失去房屋產權。

常見範例：
Foreclosure（法拍）： 借款人欠債違約，銀行強制法拍（本題情境）。
Adverse Possession（逆向占有/時效取得）： 他人長期非法佔用土地而取得產權。
Eminent Domain（政府徵收）： 政府因公共用途強制徵收民房。
Escheat（無主財產歸公）： 過世無遺囑且無合法繼承人時產權歸州政府。
Avulsion（沖蝕/地裂）： 自然災害導致土地流失。
Voluntary Alienation（自願讓與）：
定義： 業主自願（With owner's consent）將產權讓與他人。
常見範例： 出售房產（Sale / Deed）、贈與房產（Gift）、立遺囑贈與（Will / Devise）。


24.What deed provides the grantee with the least protection of any deed, while putting the least liability on the grantor? 哪一種契據給予受讓人（Grantee / 買方）最低的保護，同時給予轉讓人（Grantor / 賣方）最少的法律責任？

A. quitclaim deed(放棄權利契據) [正確答案]
B. grant deed(轉讓契據)
C. bargain and sale deed(買賣契據)
D. warranty deed(（一般）擔保契據)

【詳細解析】
核心概念：契據（Deeds）的法律責任與保護力對比
不動產交易中，不同類型的契據代表了轉讓人（Grantor）願意承擔多少法律保證與責任：
Quitclaim Deed（放棄權利契據）：
對買方（Grantee）：保護力最低（Least protection）。 它完全不提供任何產權擔保（No warranties/covenants），甚至不保證轉讓人真的擁有該房屋的所有權。買方拿到的只是轉讓人「當下可能擁有的所有權益（如有）」。
對賣方（Grantor）：法律責任最少（Least liability）。 賣方簽署後不承擔任何未來的法律追訴責任，若日後發現產權存在重大瑕疵或債務，買方完全無法向賣方索賠。
常用情境： 常用於離婚夫妻間產權轉移、修正姓名拼錯等產權清理（Clearing clouds on title）或親友間的無擔保過戶。
其他契據的保護力與責任排序：

Warranty Deed（General Warranty Deed）： 保護力最高（Greatest protection），賣方承擔最大責任（Greatest liability），包含追溯歷代前手的完整六大誓約。
Grant Deed / Special Warranty Deed： 保護力次之，僅擔保賣方自己持有期間無瑕疵。
Bargain and Sale Deed： 默示賣方擁有所有權，但不提供無負擔擔保。

考試直覺反射金句：
保護力最高、賣方責任最大 ➔ General Warranty Deed
保護力最低（Least protection）、賣方責任最少（Least liability） ➔ Quitclaim Deed


25.What purpose does the habendum clause serve in a deed?契據中的權能條款/產權處分條款（Habendum Clause）的作用是什麼？

A. It specifies what is being exchanged for the property.說明用來交換該物業的對價/條件 — 這是 Consideraton Clause
B. It defines or limits the ownership interest of the grantee.定義或限制受讓人（Grantee/買方）所獲得的所有權權益範圍) [正確答案]
C. It formally transfers the deed.正式轉讓契據/表達轉讓意圖 — 這是 Granting Clause
D. It asserts that the deed was transferred willingly. 聲明契據是出於自願轉讓的 — 這是 Acknowledgment

【詳細解析】
核心概念：Habendum Clause（權能條款 / 產權處分條款）
標誌性關鍵字： "To Have and to Hold"
在契據的法律文本中，Habendum Clause 通常緊接在轉讓條款（Granting Clause）之後，並以 "To have and to hold" 開頭。
核心功能：
它的主要作用是明確定義或限制受讓人（Grantee）所取得的產權類型與權限範圍（Quantity/Extent of interest）。
例如：說明買方取得的是完全自由繼承權（Fee Simple Absolute）、附條件繼承權（Fee Simple Defeasible），還是僅為終身物權（Life Estate，如 "to have and to hold for the duration of his life"）。
其他選項對應的契據要素（Deed Elements）：
A 說明： Consideration Clause（對價條款） — 說明買賣金額或感情對價（如 $10, or love and affection）。
C 說明： Granting Clause / Words of Conveyance（轉讓條款） — 契據中表達正式轉讓意圖的核心句子（如 "I hereby grant and convey..."）。
D 說明： Acknowledgment（公證認可） — 由公證人（Notary Public）確認轉讓人是出於自願（Willingly / Voluntary act）簽署契據。
考試直覺反射金句：
Habendum Clause ＝ 看到 "To Have and to Hold"，作用是「定義/限制權利範圍（Define or limit ownership interest）」！

26.What must happen to a deed for title to be transferred?契據必須經過什麼程序，產權（Title）才能正式發生移轉？
A. recording the deed(登錄契據 — 這是為了建立擬制告知 Constructive Notice，非產權移轉的絕對法定必要條件)
B. delivery and acceptance(交付與接受) [正確答案]
C. title search(產權檢索 — 這是買方與產權公司調查產權歷史的過程)
D. signing the deed(簽署契據 — 僅簽名但未交付給買方，產權仍未移轉)

【詳細解析】核心概念：契據生效與產權移轉（Title Transfer / Conveyance）
不動產測驗中關於契據（Deed）何時正式讓產權生效移轉，有一個絕對高頻的法律鐵律：
Delivery and Acceptance（交付與接受）：
法律生效時點： 轉讓人（Grantor）單純在契據上簽名（Signed）或進行公證（Acknowledged），產權尚未移轉。必須由轉讓人出於轉讓產權的意圖將契據「交付（Delivery）」給受讓人，且受讓人明確「接受（Acceptance）」，產權移轉（Title Transfer）才在法律上正式完成與生效。

常見考點陷阱辨析：
Recording（登錄）不是必要條件： 將契據送至縣政府登錄（Recording）的目的在於向全社會發出擬制告知（Constructive Notice）並保護買方對抗第三人主張，但登錄並非契據在買賣雙方之間生效移轉產權的必要法定步驟（即便一份有效的契據從未登錄，只要完成 Delivery & Acceptance，產權也已經合法移轉給買方）。
Signing（簽名）只是前提： 簽署是契據執行的步驟之一，但若轉讓人簽完字後將契據鎖在自己的保險箱未給買方，產權並未轉移。

考試直覺反射金句：

產權何時正式移轉（Title Transferred）？ ➔ 答案必定是 Delivery and Acceptance（交付與接受）！

27.If a property owner does not utilize their land, and if another party makes or has a claim to that land, what could potentially make the owner lose their property rights?如果土地業主不使用其土地，而另一方對該土地提出或擁有權利主張，什麼原因可能導致業主喪失其土地所有權？
A. easement by necessity(必要地役權 — 專指袋地通行權，不包含取得土地所有權)
B. encroachment(越界建築/侵占 — 一種違規事實，非取得產權的法律機制名稱)
C. dominant tenement(需役地 — 享有一切地役權條款利益的土地)
D. adverse possession (逆向占有 / 時效取得) [正確答案]
【詳細解析】🔍 核心概念：Adverse Possession（逆向占有 / 時效取得）
Adverse Possession（逆向占有） 是法律上一種透過「長期佔用他人未經管護之土地」而依法取得該土地合法所有權的非自願轉讓（Involuntary Alienation）機制。
法律邏輯與立法目的：
法律鼓勵土地的有效開發與利用。如果地主長時間閒置、忽略或不使用其土地（does not utilize their land），而第三方侵占該土地並滿足法定條件，經法定年限後，原業主可能因此喪失產權（Lose property rights）。
逆向占有的法定構成要件（記憶口訣：ONCHA）：
Open and notorious（公開且顯而易見的佔用）
Notorious/Hostile（未經地主同意且對抗地主權益）
Continuous and uninterrupted（連續不間斷的佔用，依各州規定約 5～20 年不等）
Hostile to the owner's title（敵意佔有）
Actual possession（實際占有與使用）
其他選項辨析：
A (Easement by necessity)： 僅給予袋地業主「通行使用權（Right of access）」，完全不會轉移土地的所有權（Title）。
B (Encroachment)： 越界侵占（例如鄰居的籬笆或屋簷蓋過界），屬於一種瑕疵實體狀況，若長期未處理，最終可能導致 Adverse Possession，但「導致地主失去產權」的法律機制名稱是 Adverse Possession。
C (Dominant tenement)： 指在地役權關係中，享有通行或使用權利的「需役地」土地本身。





Level10 Assessment
1.Which of these people can act as an escrow holder without an escrow license?
a broker representing one of the parties in a transaction
a salesperson representing one of the parties in a transaction
a mortgage broker
a buyer
Yes, a broker can act as an escrow holder without a license, so long as they are representing one of the parties in the transaction.


2.A property is transferred from a seller to a buyer on April 4th. At the end of the year, the buyer pays the annual tax amount of $11,160. Using the 360-day calendar, how much should be credited to the buyer for prorated property taxes? In this situation, the buyer owns closing day.
$2,883
$2,789
$3,320
$3,331
To find the monthly rate, divide the annual total by 12, which in this case equals $930. To find the daily rate, divide the taxes total by 360, which gives you 31. Remember, the day of closing is billed to the buyer. So, we have 3 months and 3 days to calculate. 930 x 3 + 31 x 3 = $2,883.


3.Costs that have been incurred but have not been paid for yet are:
accrued items
prepaid items
a debit to the buyer
non-realty items
Costs that have been incurred but have not been paid for yet are accrued items, such as some recurring special assessments and mortgage interest. These expenses have been (or are being) incurred at the time of sale, but need not be paid at the time the sale closes. Accrued items are generally debited to the seller and credited to the buyer.


4.What's the difference between the Closing Disclosure and the Loan Estimate?
The Loan Estimate is a preliminary estimate of loan costs; the Closing Disclosure is a final itemization of all services and fees.
Borrowers are given the Loan Estimate at closing and the Closing Disclosure three days after they apply for the loan.
The Loan Estimate is prepared by the seller's broker, while the Closing Disclosure is prepared by the buyer's broker.
The Closing Disclosure is a abbreviated version of the Loan Estimate.
Yes, the Loan Estimate is a preliminary estimate of loan costs; the Closing Disclosure is a final itemization of all services and fees.


5.Which party will be responsible for satisfying existing liens before closing?
the seller
the buyer
the lender
the appraiser
Yes, a seller will need to take on the expense of satisfying any existing lien on the property. In most cases, there is at least a mortgage lien that the seller will pay off with a portion of the buyer's lender's funds.


6.How will the closing agent determine how to allocate various closing costs between the parties?
They will follow what is in the escrow instructions.
They will use RESPA guidelines.
The attorneys present at closing will allocate all closing costs.
They will check the parties' credit scores and assign closing costs based on creditworthiness.
Yes, to determine how to allocate closing costs, the escrow agent will follow what is in the escrow instructions.


7.A seller's credits minus the seller's debits would equal the:
net to seller
purchase price
loan amount
closing costs
Yes, the net to seller is calculated by subtracting a seller's debits from their credits.


The buyer and the seller closed on a property on April 20th. The fees for a special assessment for the current year were paid by the seller at the beginning of the year.

April 21st through December 31st
January 1st through April 18th
May 1st through December 31st
January 1st through April 20th
Yes, the escrow holder will credit the seller (since they already paid for the whole year) and debit the buyer for fees accrued from April 21st through December 31st.

11.What would be the consequence of a lender failing to deliver a Closing Disclosure form by at least three business days prior to closing day?
The closing date gets moved.

12.What would be considered the Consumer Finance Protection Bureau's greatest contribution toward the simplification of the mortgage lending process?
the creation of the Loan Estimate form and the Closing Disclosure form
the introduction of the Uniform Residential Loan Application
mandating the use of a pre-qualification form
the regulations surrounding the use of discount points and lender credits
Yes, one of the CFPB's most noteworthy contributions is the simplification and transparency it brought to the mortgage lending process with the creation of the Loan Estimate form and its companion document, the Closing Disclosure form. These forms integrate the multiple disclosure requirements (and forms) that previously existed separately in TILA and RESPA.


Joe's lender uses an automated underwriting system. The lender approved Joe's application for a mortgage loan, but it was reviewed by a human underwriter during the process.

Joe was initially rejected by the automated underwriting system before the human underwriter approved his loan.
Joe was initially approved by the automated underwriting system and a human underwriter confirmed the automated system's approval.
Joe's file being randomly pulled for a human audit of the automated system was unrelated to the initial approval or rejection of Joe's application.
Joe's file was reviewed by a human underwriter because the loan amount met the threshold for human underwriter intervention.
Many lenders use automated underwriting systems to evaluate mortgage loan applications. In cases where the automated system rejects the application, it is flagged for review by a human underwriter to look for any additional information or extenuating circumstances that would allow the lender to approve the loan. That is what likely happened to Joe.


14.An appraisal is:

15.What is the official name of the RESPA/TILA form that serves as the closing statement?

16.When Sandra bought a property in California, she had to pay $0.55 per $500 of the purchase price to the state. What is this known as?
(譯：當 Sandra 在加州購買一處物業時，她必須按購買價格每 $500 美元支付 $0.55 美元給政府。這被稱作什麼？)
A. ad valorem property tax
(從價房產稅 — 依房屋評估價值每年定期繳納的房產稅)
B. capital gains tax
(資本利得稅 — 出售資產賺取利潤時繳納的所得稅)
C. sales tax
(銷售稅 — 購買一般商品/動產時繳納的消費稅)
D. documentary transfer tax
(契據轉讓稅 / 文件轉讓稅) [正確答案]
17.A tax bill cost $10,092. For proration purposes, what is the monthly tax expense?一份房產稅帳單總金額為 $10,092 美元。為了進行過戶比例分攤（Proration）計算，每月的稅費支出是多少？ 
$950
$27.65
$841
$28
The tax bill needs to be divided by 12, giving you a monthly expense of $841. 


18.How would the purchase price of a property appear on the closing document?物業的購買價格（成交價）在過戶結算文件（Closing Document / Settlement Statement / Closing Disclosure）上會如何顯示？
A. debit to the buyer, credit to the seller
(買方的借方 / 應付金額，賣方的貸方 / 應收金額) [正確答案]
B. debit to the buyer, debit to the seller
(買方的借方，賣方的借方)
C. credit to the buyer, debit to the seller
(買方的貸方，賣方的貸方)
D. credit to the seller, credit to the buyer
(賣方的貸方，買方的貸方)
【詳細解析】核心概念：結算表中的 Debit（借方/應付）與 Credit（貸方/應收）
在過戶結算表（如 Closing Disclosure 或 HUD-1）中，Debit 與 Credit 的定義如下：
Debit（借方 / 欠款 / 支出）： 代表該人物業交易中需要支付的金額（Money Owed / Amount Due），會增加該人需帶到過戶桌上的現金量，或減少賣方淨收到的現金。
Credit（貸方 / 進帳 / 收入）： 代表該人物業交易中應收取的金額（Money Received / Amount Credited），會減少買方需支付的現金總額，或增加賣方淨收到的現金。
房屋購買價格（Purchase Price）的對應方式：
對買方而言（Debit to the Buyer）：
購買價格是買方買房必須支付的總金額（Amount Due from Buyer），因此在買方的欄位記為 Debit。
對賣方而言（Credit to the Seller）：
購買價格是賣方出售房屋應獲得進帳的收入（Amount Due to Seller），因此在賣方的欄位記為 Credit。
19.Amani is selling her house to Joe. After signing the contract, they each delivered their own set of escrow instructions to the escrow agent. How can these instructions be described?Amani 正要將她的房屋出售給 Joe。在簽署合約後，他們各自向託管代理人（Escrow Agent）交付了「各自獨立」的一套託管指示（Escrow Instructions）。這類託管指示可以被如何描述？
A. void
(無效的)
B. unilateral
(單方的 / 各自獨立的) [正確答案]
C. bilateral
(雙方的 / 共同簽署的)
D. unenforceable
(無法強制執行的)
【詳細解析】
核心概念：託管指示（Escrow Instructions）的兩種型態
在不動產過戶託管（Escrow）流程中，買賣雙方提交給託管公司（Escrow Holder）的指示文件主要有兩種形式：
Unilateral Escrow Instructions（單方託管指示）：
定義： 買方與賣方各自分別簽署並交付獨立的託管指示文件（Two separate sets of instructions）。
區域特色： 在北加州（Northern California）以及部分美國地區，實務上最常採用這種 Unilateral Instructions 形式，由買賣雙方各自向 Escrow 提出對應的指示。
法律效力： 只要兩份獨立指示的內容最終完全吻合（In agreement），託管合約依然成立且具備完全的法律約束力，並非無效（Void）或無法執行（Unenforceable）。
Bilateral Escrow Instructions（雙方託管指示）：
定義： 買賣雙方在同一份託管指示文件上共同簽字認可（Single document signed by both parties）。這在南加州（Southern California）較為普遍。
選項辨析與陷阱排除：
本題情境（"each delivered their own set"）： 題目明確指出「他們各自交付了自己的一套指示（their own set）」，因此在法律分類上屬於 Unilateral（單方的） 託管指示。
Void / Unenforceable 錯誤： 分別提交單方指示是合法且常見的過戶流程，絕非無效或無法執行的文件。

$1,050
$2,100

$900
You can divide that number by 30 to get the daily expense, which in this case is $15. So, 2 months equals $900 and 10 days equals $150.

Level11 Assessment
Seller Marcie wants to sell her lakehouse fast. To entice buyers, she is willing to pay $2,500 of the buyer's closing costs.
How will that appear in the "Calculating Cash to Close" section of the Loan Estimate form?
as "-$2,500" on the "Seller Credits" line
as "$2,500" on the "Seller Credits" line
as "$2,500" on the "Deposit" line
as "-$2,500" on the "Deposit" line
When a seller agrees to pay some of the buyer's closing costs, it will appear on the "Seller Credits" line of the "Calculating Cash to Close" section of the Loan Estimate form. It will be written out as a negative number because it is to be subtracted from the buyer's costs.




According to conventional loans' qualifying guidelines, the monthly housing expenses, plus all other monthly obligations, cannot exceed what percent of the borrower's total gross monthly income?
36%
28%
41%
45%
Yes, for manually underwritten loans, Fannie Mae will limit the debt-to-income ratio to 36%. That is, the total PITI plus the other debts cannot exceed 36% of the borrower's monthly income.

a private mortgage insurance premium

discount points

Loan approval consists of two different approvals. What are they?
approval of the buyer and approval of the property
approval of the appraisal and approval of the seller
approval of the survey and approval of the buyer
approval of the inspection and approval of the buyer
Loan approval consists of two approvals: (1) Property approval and (2) approval of the buyer’s assets, income, and credit history. When both of the approvals are complete, the loan is approved.


Leo is buying a house for $200,000 and is making a 20% down payment. He is paying for 1 discount point, $450 for a new survey, and $900 for an environmental report. Based on this, how much cash will Leo have to bring to the closing?
$42,950
$42,300
$45,300
$43,500
By adding together the costs of Leo's down payment ($40,000 – calculated using the down payment percentage and sales price), discount point (1% of the loan amount, which is the sales price minus the down payment or 0.01 x $160,000 = $1,600), the survey, and environmental report, we find that he would have to bring $42,950 to closing. $40,000 + $1,600 + $450 + $900 = $42,950.
After doing an underwriting review of Mookie's credit rating and history, the lender pre-approved him for a mortgage loan. The property value was also in line with the lender's requirements. Then, in the week before the loan was to close, Mookie bought a $40,000 motorhome that he financed over 20 years. When his lender found out about this, they decided not to extend Mookie a loan in their final approval stage.


What is the MOST LIKELY reason Mookie's lender chose not to give final approval to his mortgage loan?
The motorhome debt changed Mookie's credit profile and desirability as a loan risk.
The lender now had to view the mortgage application as a second-home loan.
The inherent danger of driving a motorhome hurt Mookie's likelihood of repaying the mortgage loan.
The lender wasn't given the chance to finance the motorhome loan as well.
In the final approval stage of a mortgage loan, a lender will take another look at the borrower's financial profile to make sure nothing significant has changed. In Mookie's case, the addition of a $40,000 debt to his financial profile was significant enough to make the lender no longer view him as a desirable loan risk.


Trevor is taking on a 30-year mortgage loan that he only plans on staying in for a few years. Would it make sense for him to pay more at closing to lower his interest rate?
It wouldn't because it would likely take several years to break even on the upfront money he spent to buy the points.
It would because the discount points paid upfront on a loan represent a greater tax advantage than a higher monthly payment would.
It would because the discount points would serve to shorten the term of his loan.
It wouldn't because the discount points wouldn't allow him to skip the first monthly payment due on the loan.
Correct. While someone who intends on staying in a mortgage loan for a long period of time might find it to their advantage to pay discount points upfront in order to bring down the interest rate on their loan, if Trevor only plans to stay in the loan for a few years, he's unlikely to recoup what he spent on points.


Why would a lender be interested in the loan-to-value ratio of a property?
because it represents the ratio of the value of the property and the amount of the loan financing the property and is an indicator of a loan's risk
because it is a measure of the total profit on the interest to be paid over the life of the loan
because their interests may shift from loan origination to real estate acquisition if the LTV ratio comes in high enough
because they need to make sure they are aligned with RESPA guidelines on LTV ratios
Yes, a lender would be interested in the loan-to-value ratio of a property because it represents the ratio of the value of the property and the actual amount (or value) of the loan financing the property expressed as a percentage. The higher the percentage of the property value the lender is loaning, the riskier the loan.

The pre-approval letter gave a complimentary description of the property.
The pre-approval letter contained a loan commitment to the buyer.

Frank applies for a loan on his pending home purchase. He has a gross annual income of $65,000. What is the maximum monthly mortgage payment (PITI) he can afford while conforming to a payment-to-income ratio of 35%?
Round to the nearest dollar.
$1,896
$22,750
$3,521
$1,795
If you know the monthly income and the desired payment-to-income ratio, this would be the formula to use to solve for monthly payment: Monthly income x Payment-to-income ratio = Monthly payment. So, in Frank's case, you divide $65,000 by 12 to get his monthly income. Then, multiply that figure by 0.35 to get the maximum monthly mortgage payment (PITI) of $1,895.83 (which rounds to $1,896).

12.Rory makes $5,000 a month and is looking at a condo with a monthly mortgage payment of $1,500.
Can he come in under the FHA maximum allowable payment-to-income ratio?
With a payment-to-income ratio of 35%, Rory would come in under the allowable FHA maximum.
With a payment-to-income ratio of 35%, Rory would NOT come in under the allowable FHA maximum.
With a payment-to-income ratio of 30%, Rory would NOT come in under the allowable FHA maximum.
With a payment-to-income ratio of 30%, Rory would come in under the allowable FHA maximum.
在不動產金融（Mortgage Financing）章節中，FHA（聯邦住房管理局擔保貸款）有一套標準的 31/43 債務比率指南（可以在有補償性因素下放寬，但基準考題均以標準值為準）：
前端比率（Front-End Ratio / Payment-to-Income Ratio）：上限為 31%
代表每月房貸支出（PITI：本金、利息、稅金、保險）不得超過稅前總收入的 31%。
後端比率（Back-End Ratio / Total Debt-to-Income Ratio）：上限為 43%
代表每月所有債務支出（房貸＋車貸＋信用卡最低應繳＋學貸等）不得超過稅前總收入的 43%。
3. 結論分析
Rory 計算出來的比率為 30%。
因為 30% 小於 FHA 的標準上限 31%（$30\% < 31\%$），所以 Rory 成功低於（come in under） FHA 規定的最大允許比率，符合貸款審核標準。

Marissa and Erik, who live in San Francisco, own community property together. This means that they are:
either registered domestic partners OR married spouses
married spouses only
registered domestic partners only
siblings only
California is a community property state. That means that property acquired during a marriage is acquired due to the shared effort of both partners. Therefore, it's equally owned by the two of them. In California, community property applies to both married spouses AND registered domestic partners.
The buyer and seller closed on a property on June 17th. The year's HOA fees were paid by the seller at the beginning of the year. How might the seller receive credit for the time the buyer occupies the home?
At closing, the buyer credits the seller with prorated HOA dues.
The seller cannot receive a credit, as the fee has already been paid without the buyer's consent.
The escrow company pays for the prorated expense.
The HOA reimburses the seller for the fee.
Proration is the act of dividing or allocating expenses between buyers and sellers based on the actual period of usage of the item or service. HOA fees are an example of a type of expense that might be prorated at closing, which would mean the buyer credits the seller with the prorated HOA fees.

$500
$50,000

The formula for finding the monthly payment when you know the income and payment-to-income ratio is Monthly income x Payment-to-income ratio = Monthly payment. So, $2,000 x 0.25 = $500.
If a borrower has a monthly PITI payment of $800 and a gross monthly income of $2,200, what is her payment-to-income ratio?
36%
27.5%
0.36
42%
If a borrower has a monthly PITI payment of $800 and a gross monthly income of $2,200, her payment-to-income ratio is 36%. The formula to solve for that is: Monthly payment ÷ Monthly income = Payment-to-income ratio. $800 ÷ $2,200 = 0.36, which is 36% when written as a percentage.
What is a loan-to-value ratio?
the amount of money being loaned compared to the value of the property
the difference between the interest rate of an ARM loan and the index value
the process of paying off a debt/mortgage in regular installments based on a fixed payment schedule
a financing method in which an owner sells their property to an investor, who then leases the property back to the original owner
A loan-to-value ratio is the amount of money being loaned compared to the value of the property.
Lucia has a monthly PITI payment of $650 and other long-term liabilities totaling $250 per month. If she makes $3,300 a month, what is her total debt-to-income ratio?
27%
12%
19%
30%
Lucia's debt-to-income ratio is 27%. You get this by using this formula: (Monthly payment + Other debt) ÷ Monthly income = Debt-to-income ratio. In Lucia's scenario, it would look like this: ($650 + $250) ÷ $3,300 = 0.27 or 27%.
What form do most lenders use to collect mortgage loan applicant information for underwriting purposes?
Uniform Residential Loan Application (URLA)
Standard Mortgage Underwriting Residential Form (SMURF)
Universal Mortgage Loan Application (UMLA)
Lender Underwriting Mortgage Loan Application (LUMLA)
When a borrower meets with a lender to apply for a mortgage loan, the underwriting form used will likely be Fannie Mae's Uniform Residential Loan Application (URLA). It's the standard form most lenders use to collect the information necessary to evaluate the loan risk their applicants represent.
Jackie took out an amortized loan of $300,000 with a 5% interest rate. Her monthly payment is $1,800. How much of her first payment will go towards the principal?
$550
$600
$1,250
$650
First, you need to calculate the amount Jackie will pay in interest by multiplying $300,000 by 0.05 and then dividing by 12, which equals $1,250. Then you subtract $1,250 from the monthly payment of $1,800, leaving $550 as the amount going towards the principal.

Level14 Assessment
Marcos owns 2 acres of land. What is the size of his property in square feet?
87,120 sq. ft.
5,280 sq. ft.
43,560 sq. ft.
21,780 sq. ft.
One acre equals 43,560 sq. ft., so we'll multiply that number by the number of acres Marcos owns, which is 2. Marcos' land measures 87,120 sq. ft.
Rubie is a licensed land surveyor. She selects a monument as her point of beginning and defines the boundaries of a property in terms of distances and compass directions, finally returning to the point of beginning. What type of legal land description has she created?
metes and bounds
government survey system
lot and block
rectangular survey system
Metes and bounds is a land survey process in which a licensed land surveyor starts at a readily identifiable point of beginning and defines the boundaries of a property in terms of distances and compass directions, finally returning to the point of beginning.
With which type of land description does a surveyor indicate direction in degrees, minutes, and seconds?
metes and bounds
rectangular survey system
lot and block system
government survey system
Correct. The metes and bounds method of land description uses descriptions like degrees, minutes, and seconds to outline the boundaries of a property.
The NE 1/4 of a section would measure how many acres?
160
640
320
80
Correct. The NE 1/4 of a section would measure 160 acres.
A surveyor references an object when reporting the elevation of a property. If you wanted to go see that object for yourself, what would you look for?
a permanent, metal benchmark
a natural monument, such as a tree
a wooden stake in the ground
a building or similar human-made structure
Surveyors report elevation by referencing benchmarks. These are permanent, metal objects placed by the U.S. Geological Survey or the U.S. Coast and Geodetic Survey.
Courtney just purchased an 8.5-acre vacant lot of land for $500,000. What was the price Courtney paid per square yard?
$12.15
$1.35
$58.82
$36.46
Correct. 1 acre = 43,560 sq. ft., so 43,560 x 8.5 = 370,260, meaning the lot is 370,260 sq. ft. Since 1 sq. yard = 9 sq. ft., 370,260 ÷ 9 = 41,140, so the lot is 41,140 sq. yards. To find the price per square yard, you'll then divide the price by the square yards. 500,000 ÷ 41,140 = 12.15, so Courtney paid $12.15 per square yard.
Charlie purchased a lot that measures 800 ft. wide by 500 ft. long. The purchase price was $850,000. What was the price Charlie paid per ACRE?
$92,592.59
$0.47
$1,062.50
$1,360
Correct. Charlie paid $92,592.59 per acre. 800 ft. x 500 ft. = 400,000 sq. ft. Since there are 43,560 sq. ft. in an acre, we'll divide the total square footage by 43,560. 400,000 sq. ft. ÷ 43,560 sq. ft./acre = 9.18 acres, so the lot is 9.18 acres. We'll then divide the total price by the acreage. $850,000 ÷ 9.18 acres = $92,592.59/acre. Charlie paid $92,592.59 per acre.
What is a survey?
the process and physical product of finding and measuring the boundaries of a piece of real estate, including the location of improvements
the required actions for the proper execution of a deed, involving delivery of the deed by the grantor and acceptance by the grantee
the involuntary transfer of title from an owner who doesn't use or inspect their land to another person who has some claim and takes possession
a deed without any warranty against encumbrances; only implies that the grantor holds the right to convey title
A survey is the process and physical product of finding and measuring the boundaries of a piece of real estate, including the location of improvements, encroachments, and easements.
Jim buys a 400 sq. yd. lot of land. What is the size of Jim's lot in square feet?
3,600 sq. ft.
3,200 sq. ft.
1,600 sq. ft.
1,200 sq. ft.
Correct. One square yard is equal to 9 sq. ft. So, 400 sq. yards x 9 = 3,600 sq. ft.
Which legal description will likely include the phrase “point of beginning”?
metes and bounds
recorded plat
rectangular survey
vertical land
The metes and bound system describes the perimeter boundaries of the parcel using measurements, directions, and distances. A metes and bounds legal description always returns to the Point of the Beginning (POB).
Which survey system uses longitude and latitude lines to map property boundaries?
government survey system
metes and bounds system
lot and block system
recorded plat system
The government survey system uses longitude and latitude lines to map property boundaries.
Rentable square footage includes a tenant's usable square footage and:
common areas
outdoor areas
a break room only accessible on one floor
the length of the air vents within the building
This is incorrect because all outdoor areas would not be included in an area's rentable square footage. Rentable square footage is the measurement of the total area that a tenant can access which includes a tenant's usable square footage and common areas.
Range columns run parallel to:
meridians
township lines
baselines
correction lines
Range columns run parallel to meridians and create six-mile ranges.
What is the legal description of a property?
a detailed description of a property that is distinct and precise enough to distinguish it from all other properties
the U.S. Postal Service’s mailing address for the property
the 911 emergency address of the property
an appraiser’s description of the identity of the property
A legal description is a detailed description of a property that is distinct and precise enough to distinguish it from all other properties.
Which of the following is a portion of a legal description?
lot number
street address
apartment number
P.O. box
A lot number is a portion of a legal description.


Level15 Assessment
Tenant Luqman is a month behind on paying rent. Landlord Kacper gives Luqman a notice to pay or quit. When the tenant refuses to pay and refuses to move out, legally Landlord Kacper can:
bring an unlawful detainer action against the tenant
wait until the tenant leaves and then change the locks
have law enforcement remove the tenant immediately
use threats to make the tenant leave
The next step in the eviction process is for the landlord to bring an unlawful detainer action. An unlawful detainer is a legal action a landlord can take against a tenant who has defaulted on their lease.
What is quiet enjoyment?
a lessee's right to possess a property without interference from the lessor
the process by which a tenant is expelled from a property
a lease in which the tenant pays a base rent rate plus all or part of the operating expenses
a deposit made to a seller showing the buyer's good faith in regards to the future performance of a sales contract
Quiet enjoyment is a lessee's right to possess a property without interference from the lessor, previous owner, or anyone else about the claim to the title.
A landlord frequently enters one of his tenant's apartments without notice just to see how the tenant is doing. This action:
violates the tenant's right to quiet enjoyment of the property
violates the tenant's right to control how the property is used
is okay as long as the landlord wrote in the lease that he would retain the right to do this
is okay since the landlord owns the property and has a right to be there
Frequent, non-essential visits to a tenant's apartment violate the tenant's right to quiet enjoyment of the property.
What is the difference between a sublease and an assignment?
In a sublease, the original tenant is still responsible for the rent. In an assignment, the new tenant takes over responsibility.
In a sublease, the landlord can terminate the contract at any time. In an assignment, they cannot.
In a sublease, the new tenant is responsible for all costs. In an assignment, the new tenant pays the old tenant, and they pay the landlord.
In a sublease, the original space is reconfigured to fit two tenants. In an assignment, the tenants share the original space as is.
In a sublease, the original tenant is still responsible for the rent. In an assignment, the new tenant takes over responsibility. In other words, a sublease does not release the original tenant from responsibility, while an assignment does.
Which of the following individuals is exempt from FEHA?
Cathy, who owns and occupies her home and rents to one person at a time
Nichole, who rents out units in an apartment complex
Sierra, who is selling her condo
Hollie, who is selling her single-family home
The main type of person who is exempt from FEHA is an owner who rents their single-family, owner-occupied home to one individual, though they must still follow FEHA's rules on not making discriminatory statements, notices, or advertisements.
In California, an automatic renewal clause in a lease must:
be in bold font
be in a separate addendum
not be included; automatic renewal clauses are illegal in California
give the tenant the first right to purchase the property
When an automatic renewal clause is in a lease, it means the lease will be renewed (either on a month-to-month basis or on some other specified terms) without action taken by the tenant or landlord. In California, this type of automatic renewal clause in a lease must be in bold font.
In California, an urban property can't be rented for more than:
99 years
99 months
51 years
51 months
In California, an urban property cannot be rented for more than 99 years and a rural property cannot be rented for more than 51 years.
Which California law protects tenants and employees from both employment and housing discrimination based on their belonging to select groups?
Fair Employment and Housing Act
Free Establishments and Homes Act
Free and Equal Housing Act
Equal Opportunity Employment and Homes Act
California's Fair Employment and Housing Act (FEHA) protects tenants and employees from both employment and housing discrimination based on race or color, ancestry or national origin, age, disability, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity or expression, medical condition, marital status, and being in the military or a veteran.
When do tenants have the option of purchasing a property under a lease-option agreement?
on the date agreed upon in the contract
once the owner decides to sell
at the beginning of the lease period
once their monthly lease payments add up to the purchase price of the property
A lease with option to buy is a contract in which the buyer can lease a property for a period of time (usually a year) and then have an option to buy the property at a time designated in the contract.
Randy is renting his basement apartment to Josh, his son, who is a full-time college student. There is no formal lease involved, no specified termination date or rental period established, and Josh even skips payments now and then. What type of leasehold estate best describes this arrangement?
estate at will
estate for years
estate from period to period
estate at sufferance
The lack of a formal lease, specified rental period, or termination date, along with the occasional lapse in rental payment, would indicate that Randy has provided an estate at will to Josh.
The landlord of an apartment complex refuses to rent an apartment to a couple because they are not married. The landlord's actions are:
unacceptable because they are violating the Fair Employment and Housing Act
acceptable because landlords have the authority to choose suitable tenants for their properties
unacceptable because they are violating the Code of Ethics
acceptable as long as the landlord denies leases to all unmarried couples
The landlord's actions are unacceptable because they violate California's Fair Employment and Housing Act (FEHA) by discriminating against the couple based on their marital status. FEHA protects tenants and employees from both employment and housing discrimination based on race or color, ancestry or national origin, age, disability, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, medical condition, marital status, and military status (being an active member or a veteran).
Sally has been leasing a property for the past three years. Sally's landlord has decided to use the property for something else when her lease is up. What type of estate will be created if Sally refuses to leave after her lease is terminated?
tenancy at sufferance
tenant's estate
estate from period to period
tension estate
Yes, Sally's landlord would create a tenancy at sufferance, also known as an estate at sufferance. A tenancy at sufferance occurs when a tenant remains in possession of or continues to occupy a property beyond their lease term, without consent from the landlord.
Landlord Hubert uses his tenant's security deposit to repair damages to the apartment they rented for a year. Because Landlord Hubert used the security deposit funds, he must give the tenant a detailed document that shows all the charges within:
21 days after the tenant moves out
51 days after the tenant moves out
seven days after he spends the funds
one year after he spends the funds
If a landlord uses part or all of the security deposit to cover expenses, they must give the tenant a detailed document that shows all the charges at least 21 days after the tenant moves out. This is also the timeframe for which they must return the security deposit funds to the tenant.
Which of these is not something that would be included in a typical residential lease?
the option to buy at a future, specified date
the length of the lease
description of the premises
signature of the lessor
Only a lease with an option to buy will include the option to buy at a future, specified date. The length of the lease, description of the premises, and signature of the lessor are all required elements that every lease must include.
Tayshia is the landlord of a small apartment complex. When she gets a new tenant, she must:
notify the tenant that pesticides are used every month for pest control purposes
remove any lead-based paint from the unit the tenant will rent
provide the tenant with information about utility providers in the area
apply for a permit to demolish the apartment complex
Yes, landlords are required to inform tenants when pesticides are used regularly for pest control purposes on a rental property.

Level16 Assessment

What is a quitclaim deed?
a type of deed that conveys any interest, title, or right to a parcel of land the grantor has at the time the deed is executed
a deed without any warranty against encumbrances; only implies that the grantor holds the right to convey title
the voluntary gift of one’s land to the government; also known as dedication by deed
the required actions for the proper execution of a deed, involving delivery of the deed by the grantor and acceptance by the grantee
A quitclaim deed is a deed that conveys any interest, title, or right to a parcel of land the grantor has at the time the deed is executed.
"T2S R2W of Meridian 24" is a way to identify a section of property using which method of land surveying?
government survey system
metes and bounds
recorded plat method
lot and block
The government survey system uses the global lines of longitude and latitude to create a surveyed grid of meridians, base lines, townships, and ranges to describe a piece of land. "T2S R2W of Meridian 24" is a way to identify a section of property using the government survey system.
Gussie Fink-Nottle is renting Lady Edith's Shoreditch condo month-to-month. What kind of estate does Gussie have?
estate from period to period
life estate
estate for years
tenancy at sufferance
Gussie's month-to-month lease is an example of an estate from period to period.
Fiona's lender will be evaluating her for a mortgage loan. To do this, the lender will consider things like her long-term debt, the anticipated monthly mortgage payment, and her income. They have two formulas or measures they will look at.
What are those formulas and how are they collectively known?
Payment-to-income ratio and debt-to-income ratio are the two formulas or measures collectively known as the qualifying ratios.
Debt-to-income ratio and asset-to-debt ratio are the two formulas or measures collectively known as the qualifying ratios.
Payment-to-income ratio and debt-to-income ratio are the two formulas or measures collectively known as loan-to-value ratios.
Loan-to-asset ratio and asset-to-debt ratio are the two formulas or measures collectively known as the mortgage ratios.
The payment-to-income ratio and debt-to-income ratio are the two formulas or measures collectively known as the qualifying ratios. They provide lenders with a way to evaluate a borrower's ability to take on the monthly mortgage payments of the loan being considered for approval.
Hannah took out an interest-only loan for $140,000 with an interest rate of 4.9%. How much will her monthly interest payment be?
$571.67
$6,860
$140,000
$1,245.47
Annual interest ÷ 12 months = Monthly interest. To find annual interest, we multiply the principal by the interest rate: $140,000 x 0.049 = $6,860. $6,860 ÷ 12 months = $571.67.
Choose the survey method that could result in this legal description:
"Beginning at the intersection of the north boundary of Ace Dr. and the east boundary of Robot Rd.; proceed due north along the east line of Robot Rd. 120 ft. to its intersection with the center line of Blue Creek; then proceeding northeasterly 72 degrees along the center line of Blue Creek 408 ft. to the oak tree; then due south 200 ft. to the benchmark; then proceeding due west along the northern boundary of Ace Dr. 400 ft., returning to the point of beginning."
metes and bounds
rectangular survey system
lot and block
government survey system
This type of legal land description is created using the metes and bounds method. Telltale signs are the way it starts and ends with a point of beginning, references landmarks and benchmarks, and gives directions and distances to outline the perimeter of the property.
In California, what is the maximum amount a landlord can charge for a security deposit?
the equivalent of one month's rent
the equivalent of one-and-a-half months' rent
There is no legal maximum.
the equivalent of three months' rent
In California, the maximum amount that a security deposit can be is the equivalent of one month's rent.
Which of the following statements is true regarding ostensible agency and agency by estoppel?
They both have to do with when a third party is given the mistaken impression that agency exists between two other individuals.
Agency by estoppel never occurs in an environment of ostensible agency.
They are both forms of express agency.
Ostensible agency can only occur after an environment of agency by estoppel has been created.
Ostensible agency is created when the actions or words of one or two individuals lead a third party to believe that agency exists between those two individuals and the individuals responsible do nothing to correct that impression. Agency by estoppel is the legal concept that bars the responsible individuals from trying to claim after-the-fact that agency between them did not exist.
What is NOT typically included in a property manager's property marketing plan?
an appraisal of the property
a market analysis
a budget
an outline of marketing tactics
A marketing plan should include a market analysis, a budget, and an outline of marketing tactics (i.e., a budget for how much money will be spent on each marketing strategy).
Which is an example of a voluntary lien?
a borrower offering their property as security for a loan
a contractor seeking payment for roof repair work done
a lien determined by a judgment from a court
a federal tax lien imposed by the IRS
When a person takes an action that places a lien on their own property, the lien is considered voluntary. Mortgage lenders usually require security for a loan, so borrowers often provide the real estate as security, thereby creating a voluntary lien.
